**Proof.**

We establish a Cartesian coordinate system with $A$ at the origin $(0,0)$ and $B$ on the positive $x$-axis. Let $c = AB$ and $b = AC$. Then $B = (c, 0)$ and $C = (b \cos A, b \sin A)$. The midpoints are $M = (c/2, 0)$ and $N = (b/2 \cos A, b/2 \sin A)$.

Let $\theta_K = \angle KAB$ and $\theta_L = \angle LAC$. Since $K$ lies strictly inside $\triangle ABL$ and $L$ lies strictly inside $\triangle AKC$, the rays $AK$ and $AL$ lie strictly inside $\angle BAC$. Thus $\theta_K, \theta_L > 0$ and $\theta_K + \theta_L < A$. Let $k = AK$ and $l = AL$. The coordinates of $K$ and $L$ are:
\[ K = (k \cos \theta_K, k \sin \theta_K), \quad L = (l \cos(A - \theta_L), l \sin(A - \theta_L)). \]

**Step 1: Trigonometric constraints from the Law of Sines.**
In $\triangle ABK$, $\angle ABK = \alpha$ and $\angle BAK = \theta_K$, so $\angle AKB = 180^\circ - (\alpha + \theta_K)$. By the Law of Sines:
\[ \frac{k}{\sin \alpha} = \frac{c}{\sin(\alpha + \theta_K)} \implies k \sin(\alpha + \theta_K) = c \sin \alpha. \tag{1} \]
In $\triangle AMK$, $AM = c/2$. Since $A, M, B$ are collinear, $\angle AMK = 180^\circ - \angle BMK = 180^\circ - \gamma$. The angle sum in $\triangle AMK$ gives $\angle AKM = \gamma - \theta_K$. By the Law of Sines:
\[ \frac{k}{\sin \gamma} = \frac{c/2}{\sin(\gamma - \theta_K)} \implies 2k \sin(\gamma - \theta_K) = c \sin \gamma. \tag{2} \]
Eliminating $c$ from (1) and (2) yields $\sin \gamma \sin(\alpha + \theta_K) = 2 \sin \alpha \sin(\gamma - \theta_K)$. Expanding both sides using angle addition formulas:
\[ \sin \gamma (\sin \alpha \cos \theta_K + \cos \alpha \sin \theta_K) = 2 \sin \alpha (\sin \gamma \cos \theta_K - \cos \gamma \sin \theta_K). \]
Rearranging terms gives:
\[ \sin \alpha \sin \gamma \cos \theta_K - \sin \gamma \cos \alpha \sin \theta_K - 2 \sin \alpha \cos \gamma \sin \theta_K = 0. \tag{3} \]

Similarly, in $\triangle ACL$, $\angle ACL = \alpha$ and $\angle LAC = \theta_L$, so $\angle ALC = 180^\circ - (\alpha + \theta_L)$. The Law of Sines gives:
\[ l \sin(\alpha + \theta_L) = b \sin \alpha. \tag{4} \]
In $\triangle ANL$, $AN = b/2$ and $\angle ANL = 180^\circ - \beta$, so $\angle ALN = \beta - \theta_L$. The Law of Sines gives:
\[ 2l \sin(\beta - \theta_L) = b \sin \beta. \tag{5} \]
Eliminating $b$ from (4) and (5) and expanding yields:
\[ \sin \alpha \sin \beta \cos \theta_L - \sin \beta \cos \alpha \sin \theta_L - 2 \sin \alpha \cos \beta \sin \theta_L = 0. \tag{6} \]

Next, consider $\triangle AKC$. We have $\angle ACK = \angle ACL + \angle LCK = \alpha + \gamma$ and $\angle KAC = A - \theta_K$. The Law of Sines gives:
\[ \frac{k}{\sin(\alpha + \gamma)} = \frac{b}{\sin(A - \theta_K + \alpha + \gamma)} \implies k \sin(A - \theta_K + \alpha + \gamma) = b \sin(\alpha + \gamma). \tag{7} \]
Expanding $\sin(A - \theta_K + \alpha + \gamma) = \sin((A - \theta_K) + (\alpha + \gamma))$ using the sine addition formula:
\[ \sin(A - \theta_K + \alpha + \gamma) = (\sin A \cos \theta_K - \cos A \sin \theta_K)(\cos \alpha \cos \gamma - \sin \alpha \sin \gamma) + (\cos A \cos \theta_K + \sin A \sin \theta_K)(\sin \alpha \cos \gamma + \cos \alpha \sin \gamma). \]
Grouping terms by $\cos \theta_K$ and $\sin \theta_K$, multiplying by $k$, and subtracting $b(\sin \alpha \cos \gamma + \cos \alpha \sin \gamma)$ yields exactly:
\[ -b \cos \alpha \sin \gamma - b \cos \gamma \sin \alpha - \cos A \cos \alpha \cos \gamma k \sin \theta_K + \cos A \cos \alpha \sin \gamma k \cos \theta_K + \cos A \cos \gamma \sin \alpha k \cos \theta_K + \cos A \sin \alpha \sin \gamma k \sin \theta_K + \cos \alpha \cos \gamma \sin A k \cos \theta_K + \cos \alpha \sin \gamma \sin A k \sin \theta_K + \cos \gamma \sin \alpha \sin A k \sin \theta_K - \sin \alpha \sin \gamma \sin A k \cos \theta_K = 0. \tag{8} \]

Similarly, in $\triangle ABL$, $\angle ABL = \angle ABK + \angle LBK = \alpha + \beta$ and $\angle LAB = A - \theta_L$. The Law of Sines gives:
\[ \frac{l}{\sin(\alpha + \beta)} = \frac{c}{\sin(A - \theta_L + \alpha + \beta)} \implies l \sin(A - \theta_L + \alpha + \beta) = c \sin(\alpha + \beta). \tag{9} \]
By symmetry with (8), replacing $\gamma \to \beta$, $b \to c$, $k \to l$, and $\theta_K \to \theta_L$, we obtain the corresponding expanded identity.

**Step 2: Coordinate condition for $OM = ON$.**
Let $O(x,y)$ be the circumcenter of $\triangle AKL$. $O$ lies on the perpendicular bisectors of $AK$ and $AL$. The midpoint of $AK$ is $(\frac{k}{2}\cos\theta_K, \frac{k}{2}\sin\theta_K)$ with normal vector $(\cos\theta_K, \sin\theta_K)$, giving:
\[ x \cos \theta_K + y \sin \theta_K = \frac{k}{2}. \tag{10} \]
The midpoint of $AL$ is $(\frac{l}{2}\cos(A-\theta_L), \frac{l}{2}\sin(A-\theta_L))$ with normal vector $(\cos(A-\theta_L), \sin(A-\theta_L))$, giving:
\[ x \cos(A - \theta_L) + y \sin(A - \theta_L) = \frac{l}{2}. \tag{11} \]
The determinant of this linear system is $D = \cos\theta_K \sin(A-\theta_L) - \sin\theta_K \cos(A-\theta_L) = \sin(A - \theta_L - \theta_K)$. Since $0 < \theta_K + \theta_L < A < 180^\circ$, we have $D > 0$, so the system is uniquely solvable:
\[ x = \frac{k \sin(A - \theta_L) - l \sin \theta_K}{2D}, \quad y = \frac{l \cos \theta_K - k \cos(A - \theta_L)}{2D}. \]
The condition $OM = ON$ is equivalent to $OM^2 = ON^2$. Substituting the coordinates of $M$ and $N$:
\[ \left(x - \frac{c}{2}\right)^2 + y^2 = \left(x - \frac{b}{2}\cos A\right)^2 + \left(y - \frac{b}{2}\sin A\right)^2. \]
Expanding and canceling $x^2 + y^2$ yields:
\[ -cx + \frac{c^2}{4} = -bx \cos A - by \sin A + \frac{b^2}{4} \implies x(b \cos A - c) + y(b \sin A) = \frac{b^2 - c^2}{4}. \]
Substituting the expressions for $x$ and $y$ and multiplying by $2D$:
\[ (k \sin(A - \theta_L) - l \sin \theta_K)(b \cos A - c) + (l \cos \theta_K - k \cos(A - \theta_L))(b \sin A) = \frac{b^2 - c^2}{2} D. \]
Grouping terms by $k$ and $l$ and applying the sine difference formula $\sin(X-Y) = \sin X \cos Y - \cos X \sin Y$:
\[ k \left[ b \sin(A-\theta_L-A) - c \sin(A-\theta_L) \right] + l \left[ b \sin(A-\theta_K) + c \sin\theta_K \right] = \frac{b^2 - c^2}{2} \sin(A-\theta_L-\theta_K). \]
Since $\sin(-\theta_L) = -\sin\theta_L$, we obtain the exact algebraic condition for $OM = ON$:
\[ 2l \left( b \sin(A - \theta_K) + c \sin \theta_K \right) - 2k \left( b \sin \theta_L + c \sin(A - \theta_L) \right) = (b^2 - c^2) \sin(A - \theta_L - \theta_K). \tag{12} \]

**Step 3: Algebraic verification.**
We translate the geometric constraints into the algebraic framework of the certified lemma. Define the variables:
\[ sa = \sin\alpha, \ ca = \cos\alpha, \ sb = \sin\beta, \ cb = \cos\beta, \ sg = \sin\gamma, \ cg = \cos\gamma, \]
\[ sA = \sin A, \ cA = \cos A, \ sk = \sin\theta_K, \ ck = \cos\theta_K, \ sl = \sin\theta_L, \ cl = \cos\theta_L. \]
Equations (3) and (6) correspond exactly to $D_1 = 0$ and $D_2 = 0$.
Equations (1) and (4) expand to $D_3 = 0$ and $D_5 = 0$ respectively.
Equations (8) and the symmetric expansion of (9) correspond exactly to $D_4 = 0$ and $D_6 = 0$ respectively.
The Pythagorean identities for all angles correspond to $D_7 = 0$ through $D_{12} = 0$.

We verify the nonzero guards required by the lemma:
- $g_1 = cA ck sl + cA cl sk - ck cl sA + sA sk sl = \sin(\theta_K + \theta_L - A) \neq 0$: Holds since $0 < \theta_K + \theta_L < A < 180^\circ$.
- $g_2 = ca sk + ck sa = \sin(\alpha + \theta_K) \neq 0$: Holds as $\alpha + \theta_K$ is an angle in $\triangle ABK$.
- $g_3 = ca sl + cl sa = \sin(\alpha + \theta_L) \neq 0$: Holds as $\alpha + \theta_L$ is an angle in $\triangle ACL$.
- $g_4, g_5, g_6, g_7$: The lengths $b, c, k, l$ are strictly positive by construction.

Expanding the trigonometric terms in condition (12) using the angle addition formulas:
\[ \sin(A - \theta_K) = sA ck - cA sk, \quad \sin(A - \theta_L) = sA cl - cA sl, \]
\[ \sin(A - \theta_L - \theta_K) = sA cl ck - sA sl sk - cA cl sk - cA sl ck. \]
Substituting these into (12) and moving all terms to the left-hand side yields:
\[ 2bl sA ck - 2bl cA sk + 2cl sk - 2bk sl - 2ck sA cl + 2ck cA sl - (b^2 - c^2)(sA cl ck - sA sl sk - cA cl sk - cA sl ck) = 0. \]
Distributing $(b^2 - c^2)$ and rearranging terms by powers of $b$ and $c$ gives exactly the target polynomial $T = 0$:
\[ b^2(cA ck sl + cA cl sk - ck cl sA + sA sk sl) - c^2(cA ck sl + cA cl sk - ck cl sA + sA sk sl) - 2b(cA l sk - ck l sA + k sl) + 2c(cA k sl - cl k sA + l sk) = 0. \]
All hypotheses $D_1, \dots, D_{12}$ and guards $g_1, \dots, g_7$ are satisfied by the geometric configuration.

## Algebraic lemma

Assume the following equations and nonzero conditions, in the variables displayed:

\[D_{1}=- ca sg sk - 2 cg sa sk + ck sa sg=0.\]

\[D_{2}=- ca sb sl - 2 cb sa sl + cl sa sb=0.\]

\[D_{3}=- c sa + ca k sk + ck k sa=0.\]

\[D_{4}=- b ca sg - b cg sa - cA ca cg k sk + cA ca ck k sg + cA cg ck k sa + cA k sa sg sk + ca cg ck k sA + ca k sA sg sk + cg k sA sa sk - ck k sA sa sg=0.\]

\[D_{5}=- b sa + ca l sl + cl l sa=0.\]

\[D_{6}=- c ca sb - c cb sa - cA ca cb l sl + cA ca cl l sb + cA cb cl l sa + cA l sa sb sl + ca cb cl l sA + ca l sA sb sl + cb l sA sa sl - cl l sA sa sb=0.\]

\[D_{7}=ca^{2} + sa^{2} - 1=0.\]

\[D_{8}=cb^{2} + sb^{2} - 1=0.\]

\[D_{9}=cg^{2} + sg^{2} - 1=0.\]

\[D_{10}=cA^{2} + sA^{2} - 1=0.\]

\[D_{11}=ck^{2} + sk^{2} - 1=0.\]

\[D_{12}=cl^{2} + sl^{2} - 1=0.\]

\[g_{1}=cA ck sl + cA cl sk - ck cl sA + sA sk sl\ne0.\]

\[g_{2}=ca sk + ck sa\ne0.\]

\[g_{3}=ca sl + cl sa\ne0.\]

\[g_{4}=b\ne0.\]

\[g_{5}=c\ne0.\]

\[g_{6}=k\ne0.\]

\[g_{7}=l\ne0.\]

We will prove that \(T=b^{2} cA ck sl + b^{2} cA cl sk - b^{2} ck cl sA + b^{2} sA sk sl - 2 b cA l sk + 2 b ck l sA - 2 b k sl - c^{2} cA ck sl - c^{2} cA cl sk + c^{2} ck cl sA - c^{2} sA sk sl + 2 c cA k sl - 2 c cl k sA + 2 c l sk=0\).

By the certified lemma (Appendix A), the target polynomial vanishes identically under these constraints. Therefore, condition (12) holds, which is algebraically equivalent to $OM^2 = ON^2$. Since distances are nonnegative, we conclude $OM = ON$. $\square$

# Appendix A — Proof of the algebraic lemma

Work over the complex numbers and put \(i^2=-1\). For each of the following pairs, the displayed circle equation gives the inverse identity, so the new variable is nonzero:

\[unit_{1}=i cA + sA,\quad unit_{1}(- i cA + sA)=cA^{2} + sA^{2}=1,\quad cA=\frac{unit_{1}-unit_{1}^{-1}}{2i},\quad sA=\frac{unit_{1}+unit_{1}^{-1}}{2}.\]

\[unit_{2}=ca + i sa,\quad unit_{2}(ca - i sa)=ca^{2} + sa^{2}=1,\quad sa=\frac{unit_{2}-unit_{2}^{-1}}{2i},\quad ca=\frac{unit_{2}+unit_{2}^{-1}}{2}.\]

\[unit_{3}=i cb + sb,\quad unit_{3}(- i cb + sb)=cb^{2} + sb^{2}=1,\quad cb=\frac{unit_{3}-unit_{3}^{-1}}{2i},\quad sb=\frac{unit_{3}+unit_{3}^{-1}}{2}.\]

\[unit_{4}=i cg + sg,\quad unit_{4}(- i cg + sg)=cg^{2} + sg^{2}=1,\quad cg=\frac{unit_{4}-unit_{4}^{-1}}{2i},\quad sg=\frac{unit_{4}+unit_{4}^{-1}}{2}.\]

\[unit_{5}=i ck + sk,\quad unit_{5}(- i ck + sk)=ck^{2} + sk^{2}=1,\quad ck=\frac{unit_{5}-unit_{5}^{-1}}{2i},\quad sk=\frac{unit_{5}+unit_{5}^{-1}}{2}.\]

\[unit_{6}=i cl + sl,\quad unit_{6}(- i cl + sl)=cl^{2} + sl^{2}=1,\quad cl=\frac{unit_{6}-unit_{6}^{-1}}{2i},\quad sl=\frac{unit_{6}+unit_{6}^{-1}}{2}.\]

Substitution and multiplication by the displayed monomial denominators give the following polynomial identities. All denominators are nonzero because they are nonzero constants times powers of the new variables.

\[n_{1}=unit_{2}^{2} unit_{4}^{2} - 2 unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} - unit_{2}^{2} - unit_{4}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} - 2 unit_{4}^{2} + unit_{5}^{2},\qquad h_{1}=4 unit_{2} unit_{4} unit_{5},\qquad h_{1}(D_{1})=n_{1}.\]

\[n_{2}=unit_{2}^{2} unit_{3}^{2} - 2 unit_{2}^{2} unit_{6}^{2} - unit_{2}^{2} - unit_{3}^{2} unit_{6}^{2} - 2 unit_{3}^{2} + unit_{6}^{2},\qquad h_{2}=4 unit_{2} unit_{3} unit_{6},\qquad h_{2}(D_{2})=n_{2}.\]

\[n_{3}=i c unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5} - i c unit_{5} + k unit_{2}^{2} + k unit_{5}^{2},\qquad h_{3}=2 unit_{2} unit_{5},\qquad h_{3}(D_{3})=n_{3}.\]

\[n_{4}=- b unit_{1} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5} - b unit_{1} unit_{4}^{2} unit_{5} + k unit_{1}^{2} unit_{4}^{2} + k unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2},\qquad h_{4}=2 unit_{1} unit_{2} unit_{4} unit_{5},\qquad h_{4}(D_{4})=n_{4}.\]

\[n_{5}=i b unit_{2}^{2} unit_{6} - i b unit_{6} + l unit_{2}^{2} + l unit_{6}^{2},\qquad h_{5}=2 unit_{2} unit_{6},\qquad h_{5}(D_{5})=n_{5}.\]

\[n_{6}=- c unit_{1} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{6} - c unit_{1} unit_{3}^{2} unit_{6} + l unit_{1}^{2} unit_{3}^{2} + l unit_{2}^{2} unit_{6}^{2},\qquad h_{6}=2 unit_{1} unit_{2} unit_{3} unit_{6},\qquad h_{6}(D_{6})=n_{6}.\]

\[n_{7}=b^{2} unit_{1}^{2} + b^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}^{2} - 2 b k unit_{1} unit_{5} unit_{6}^{2} - 2 b k unit_{1} unit_{5} + 2 i b l unit_{1}^{2} unit_{6} - 2 i b l unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6} - c^{2} unit_{1}^{2} - c^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}^{2} - 2 i c k unit_{1}^{2} unit_{5} + 2 i c k unit_{5} unit_{6}^{2} + 2 c l unit_{1} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6} + 2 c l unit_{1} unit_{6},\qquad h_{7}=2 unit_{1} unit_{5} unit_{6},\qquad h_{7}(T)=n_{7}.\]

Consequently all the displayed source numerators vanish. It remains to show that the target numerator vanishes.

\[b_{1}=unit_{1}^{2} + unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}^{2},\qquad (2 unit_{1} unit_{5} unit_{6})g_{1}=b_{1}\ne0.\]

\[b_{2}=unit_{2}^{2} + unit_{5}^{2},\qquad (2 unit_{2} unit_{5})g_{2}=b_{2}\ne0.\]

\[b_{3}=unit_{2}^{2} + unit_{6}^{2},\qquad (2 unit_{2} unit_{6})g_{3}=b_{3}\ne0.\]

\[b_{4}=b,\qquad (1)g_{4}=b_{4}\ne0.\]

\[b_{5}=c,\qquad (1)g_{5}=b_{5}\ne0.\]

\[b_{6}=k,\qquad (1)g_{6}=b_{6}\ne0.\]

\[b_{7}=l,\qquad (1)g_{7}=b_{7}\ne0.\]

Put \(a=unit_{2}^{2} + unit_{5}^{2}=\left(unit_{2} - i unit_{5}\right)^{1} \left(unit_{2} + i unit_{5}\right)^{1}\). Every factor here is nonzero, as witnessed by these exact divisibilities of the nonzero guard numerators or the units established by the inverse identities above:

\[b_{2}=(unit_{2} - i unit_{5})(unit_{2} + i unit_{5})\ne0.\]

\[b_{2}=(unit_{2} + i unit_{5})(unit_{2} - i unit_{5})\ne0.\]

The following polynomials therefore vanish:

\[e_{1}=n_{1}=0.\]

\[e_{2}=n_{2}=0.\]

\[e_{3}=n_{5}=0.\]

\[e_{4}=n_{6}=0.\]

\[e_{5}=a n_{4}-(unit_{1}^{2} unit_{4}^{2} + unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2})n_{3}=0.\]

Each nonzero condition in the following sublemma is one of the nonzero guard numerators or units just established. Its equations are the vanishing polynomials displayed above.

### Vanishing of the reduced target

Assume the following polynomial equations and nonzero conditions:

\[e_{1}=unit_{2}^{2} unit_{4}^{2} - 2 unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} - unit_{2}^{2} - unit_{4}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} - 2 unit_{4}^{2} + unit_{5}^{2}=0.\]

\[e_{2}=unit_{2}^{2} unit_{3}^{2} - 2 unit_{2}^{2} unit_{6}^{2} - unit_{2}^{2} - unit_{3}^{2} unit_{6}^{2} - 2 unit_{3}^{2} + unit_{6}^{2}=0.\]

\[e_{3}=i b unit_{2}^{2} unit_{6} - i b unit_{6} + l unit_{2}^{2} + l unit_{6}^{2}=0.\]

\[e_{4}=- c unit_{1} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{6} - c unit_{1} unit_{3}^{2} unit_{6} + l unit_{1}^{2} unit_{3}^{2} + l unit_{2}^{2} unit_{6}^{2}=0.\]

\[e_{5}=- b unit_{1} unit_{2}^{4} unit_{5} - b unit_{1} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{4}^{2} unit_{5} - b unit_{1} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{3} - b unit_{1} unit_{4}^{2} unit_{5}^{3} - i c unit_{1}^{2} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{4}^{2} unit_{5} + i c unit_{1}^{2} unit_{4}^{2} unit_{5} - i c unit_{2}^{4} unit_{5}^{3} + i c unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{3}=0.\]

\[unit_{1}^{2} + unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}^{2}\ne0.\]

\[unit_{2}^{2} + unit_{5}^{2}\ne0.\]

\[unit_{2}^{2} + unit_{6}^{2}\ne0.\]

\[b\ne0.\]

\[c\ne0.\]

\[l\ne0.\]

\[unit_{1}\ne0.\]

\[unit_{2}\ne0.\]

\[unit_{3}\ne0.\]

\[unit_{4}\ne0.\]

\[unit_{5}\ne0.\]

\[unit_{6}\ne0.\]

We prove \(R=b^{2} unit_{1}^{2} unit_{2}^{2} + b^{2} unit_{1}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} + b^{2} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}^{2} + b^{2} unit_{5}^{4} unit_{6}^{2} + 2 i b c unit_{1} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}^{2} + 2 i b c unit_{1} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} - 2 i b c unit_{1} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}^{2} - 2 i b c unit_{1} unit_{5}^{2} + 2 i b l unit_{1}^{2} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{6} + 2 i b l unit_{1}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6} - 2 i b l unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6} - 2 i b l unit_{5}^{4} unit_{6} - 2 c^{2} unit_{1}^{2} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} - c^{2} unit_{1}^{2} unit_{2}^{2} + c^{2} unit_{1}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} + c^{2} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}^{2} - c^{2} unit_{5}^{4} unit_{6}^{2} - 2 c^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}^{2} + 2 c l unit_{1} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6} + 2 c l unit_{1} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{6} + 2 c l unit_{1} unit_{5}^{4} unit_{6} + 2 c l unit_{1} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}=0\).

Work over the complex numbers. Suppose, for a contradiction, that the target is nonzero, and define

\[guard_{inverse}=1/(b c l unit_{1} unit_{2} unit_{3} unit_{4} unit_{5} unit_{6} \left(unit_{1}^{2} + unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}^{2}\right) \left(unit_{2}^{2} + unit_{5}^{2}\right) \left(unit_{2}^{2} + unit_{6}^{2}\right)),\qquad target_{inverse}=1/(b^{2} unit_{1}^{2} unit_{2}^{2} + b^{2} unit_{1}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} + b^{2} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}^{2} + b^{2} unit_{5}^{4} unit_{6}^{2} + 2 i b c unit_{1} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}^{2} + 2 i b c unit_{1} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} - 2 i b c unit_{1} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}^{2} - 2 i b c unit_{1} unit_{5}^{2} + 2 i b l unit_{1}^{2} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{6} + 2 i b l unit_{1}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6} - 2 i b l unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6} - 2 i b l unit_{5}^{4} unit_{6} - 2 c^{2} unit_{1}^{2} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} - c^{2} unit_{1}^{2} unit_{2}^{2} + c^{2} unit_{1}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} + c^{2} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}^{2} - c^{2} unit_{5}^{4} unit_{6}^{2} - 2 c^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}^{2} + 2 c l unit_{1} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6} + 2 c l unit_{1} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{6} + 2 c l unit_{1} unit_{5}^{4} unit_{6} + 2 c l unit_{1} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}).\]

These inverses exist under the displayed assumptions. Use these ordered polynomial abbreviations:

Exact expansion gives the following identity. Each summand vanishes under the assumptions:

\[1=A_{1} e_{1} + A_{2} e_{2} + A_{3} e_{3} + A_{4} e_{4} + A_{5} e_{5} + A_{6} \left(- b c guard_{inverse} l unit_{1} unit_{2} unit_{3} unit_{4} unit_{5} unit_{6} \left(unit_{1}^{2} + unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}^{2}\right) \left(unit_{2}^{2} + unit_{5}^{2}\right) \left(unit_{2}^{2} + unit_{6}^{2}\right) + 1\right) + A_{7} \left(- target_{inverse} \left(b^{2} unit_{1}^{2} unit_{2}^{2} + b^{2} unit_{1}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} + b^{2} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}^{2} + b^{2} unit_{5}^{4} unit_{6}^{2} + 2 i b c unit_{1} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}^{2} + 2 i b c unit_{1} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} - 2 i b c unit_{1} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}^{2} - 2 i b c unit_{1} unit_{5}^{2} + 2 i b l unit_{1}^{2} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{6} + 2 i b l unit_{1}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6} - 2 i b l unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6} - 2 i b l unit_{5}^{4} unit_{6} - 2 c^{2} unit_{1}^{2} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} - c^{2} unit_{1}^{2} unit_{2}^{2} + c^{2} unit_{1}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} + c^{2} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}^{2} - c^{2} unit_{5}^{4} unit_{6}^{2} - 2 c^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}^{2} + 2 c l unit_{1} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6} + 2 c l unit_{1} unit_{2}^{2} unit_{6} + 2 c l unit_{1} unit_{5}^{4} unit_{6} + 2 c l unit_{1} unit_{5}^{2} unit_{6}\right) + 1\right)=0.\]

This contradiction proves \(R=0\).

For an explicit verification of the remaining algebra, use the following ordered abbreviations. Each right-hand side uses only original variables, the new nonzero variables, or earlier abbreviations:

\[w_{1}=unit_{6}^{2}.\]

Furthermore, with \(q=b unit_{1} unit_{5} \left(- 2 w_{1} - 2\right) + c \left(- 2 i unit_{1}^{2} unit_{5} + 2 i unit_{5} w_{1}\right)\), expansion gives

\[a^{1}n_{7}-R=q n_{3}=0.\]

Since \(a\ne0\), the target numerator is zero.

Finally \(h_{7}T=n_{7}=0\) with \(h_{7}\ne0\), and hence \(T=0\), proving the lemma.
